=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer sequence A = (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) of length N.
You can perform the following operation any number of times, possibly zero:

- Choose an integer pair (i, j) satisfying 1 \leq i \lt j \leq N, and replace A_i with A_i + 1 and A_j with A_j - 1.

Determine whether it is possible to make A a non-decreasing sequence through the operations.
You are given T test cases. Solve each of them.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format. Here, \mathrm{case}_i denotes the i-th test case.
T
\mathrm{case}_1
\mathrm{case}_2
\vdots
\mathrm{case}_T

Each test case is given in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print T lines. The i-th line should contain the answer for the i-th test case.
For each test case, if it is possible to make A a non-decreasing sequence through the operations, print Yes; otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1 \leq T \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
- The sum of N over all test cases is at most 2 \times 10^5.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3
3
1 7 5
2
9 0
10
607 495 419 894 610 636 465 331 925 724

Sample Output 1

Yes
No
Yes

In the first test case, you can make A into a non-decreasing sequence by performing the following operations:

- Choose (i, j) = (1, 2). After the operation, A is (2, 6, 5).
- Choose (i, j) = (1, 2). After the operation, A is (3, 5, 5).

In the second test case, you cannot make A into a non-decreasing sequence no matter how you perform the operations.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

This criterion seems strong. Need prove balanced sequence maximizes prefix sums among nondecreasing integer sequences with fixed sum S. Let's formalize.

Let S = qN + r, 0 <= r < N. Define B^*_i = q for i <= N-r, B^*_i = q+1 for i > N-r. This is nondecreasing, sum S. For any nondecreasing integer sequence B with sum S, show for all k, sum_{i=1}^k B_i <= sum_{i=1}^k B^*_i.

Proof option: Since B nondecreasing, for any k, if B_k <= q, then Q_k <= k q. If B_k >= q+1, then B_{k+1..N} >= q+1, so suffix sum >= (N-k)(q+1). Total S = qN+r. Then Q_k = S - suffix <= qN+r - (N-k)(q+1) = kq + r - (N-k) = kq - (N-k-r). Need compare to Q^*_k.

Let's compute Q^*_k:
If k <= N-r: Q^*_k = kq.
If k > N-r: Q^*_k = (N-r)q + (k-(N-r))(q+1) = kq + k - (N-r) = kq + k - N + r.

Now for arbitrary B:
Case B_k <= q: Since first k <= B_k <= q, Q_k <= kq. If k <= N-r, Q^*_k=kq, done. If k > N-r, Q^*_k = kq + positive, so Q_k <= kq <= Q^*_k. Good.
Case B_k >= q+1: Since suffix from k+1 to N >= B_k? Actually B_{k+1} >= B_k >= q+1, so suffix sum >= (N-k)(q+1). Need handle.

If k <= N-r, Q^*_k=kq. From suffix bound, Q_k <= kq + r - (N-k) = kq - (N-k-r). Since k <= N-r => N-k >= r, so this <= kq. Thus Q_k <= kq = Q^*_k. Good. If k > N-r, Q^*_k = kq + k - N + r, and bound equals that. So done. Nice.

Implementation details:
import sys
def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]; idx=1
    out=[]
    for _ in range(t):
        n=data[idx]; idx+=1
        A = data[idx:idx+n]; idx+=n
        S = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(S, n)
        # n-r is count of q values
        base_count = n - r
        pref=0
        ok=True
        for k in range(1, n): # compare first n-1
            pref += A[k-1]
            if k <= base_count:
                limit = q * k
            else:
                limit = q * k + (k - base_count)
            if pref > limit:
                ok=False
                break
        out.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
if __name__=="__main__": main()

Let B nondecreasing integers, sum S=qN+r. Define C_i = q for i<=N-r, q+1 for i>N-r. Need show for all k, ∑_{i=1}^k B_i ≤ ∑_{i=1}^k C_i.

Proof using "uncrossing" or "majorization": C is the unique vector with minimal range. For any nondecreasing B, if B_k ≤ q, then first k ≤ q, so Q_k≤kq. If k≤N-r, C_prefix=kq; if k>N-r, C_prefix≥kq. So done. If B_k ≥ q+1, then last N-k elements ≥ q+1, so Q_k = S - suffix ≤ S - (N-k)(q+1) = kq + r - N + k. If k≤N-r, C_prefix=kq; need show kq + r - N + k ≤ kq iff k ≤ N-r, yes. If k>N-r, C_prefix = kq + k - N + r, equal. Thus Q_k≤C_prefix. This proof uses B_k threshold. What if B_k not integer? B integer yes. If q maybe 0, works. If B_k ≤ q vs ≥q+1 covers all integers. Now, reachability theorem: Need be absolutely certain prefix condition is sufficient for integer vectors with operation moving one unit left. Let's prove or find counterexample.

We have A, B nonnegative integers, sum S, and for all k, P^B_k ≥ P^A_k. Is B reachable from A by operations i<j? This is equivalent to B can be obtained by moving mass left. There is a known necessary and sufficient condition: For all suffixes, B suffix sum ≤ A suffix sum. Since prefix condition equivalent. To construct, process from right to left:
Let j = N down to 2. At index j, if current A_j > B_j, we need move surplus A_j - B_j units from j to some earlier positions. But if current A_j < B_j, that means j has received units from even later? Since we process from right to left, we might have moved units into j from later positions, so current A_j could exceed B_j. We can move surplus left. We need ensure there is enough deficit earlier. The total surplus of suffix? Let's define D_k = P^B_k - P^A_k ≥0. D_N=0. For each k, D_k is net units that must cross cut between k and k+1 from right to left. Since D_k≥0. We can realize by moving D_k units across each cut. Operations from j to i cross cuts i..j-1. If we choose for each unit its final position, the number crossing cut k is D_k. Since D_k nonnegative and D_N=0. Need route units from original positions to final positions q≤p. This is a transportation problem on a line with supplies A at origins, demands B at destinations, allowed direction left. Feasibility iff for all prefixes, demand in prefix ≥ supply in prefix (because units cannot move right; supply in prefix cannot satisfy demand outside prefix except by moving right, not allowed; so demand outside prefix must be supplied by supply outside prefix, equivalently demand prefix ≥ supply prefix). This is exactly Hall. Since supplies/demands integers, feasible. So theorem holds. Good.

Let's maybe write code:
import sys

def solve():
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    T = next(it)
    ans = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = next(it)
        A = [next(it) for _ in range(N)]
        S = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(S, N)
        zero_count = N - r  # number of q's
        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, N):
            pref += A[k-1]
            if k <= zero_count:
                limit = q * k
            else:
                limit = q * k + (k - zero_count)
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break
        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    print("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

If using iter(map(int, ...)), next(it) for each A. Need handle StopIteration? no.

If k ≤ N-r: ∑_{i=1}^k (A_i - q) ≤ 0.
If k > N-r: ∑_{i=1}^k (A_i - q) ≤ k - (N-r) = k - N + r.
Equivalently ∑_{i=1}^k (A_i - q) ≤ max(0, k - (N-r)).
But code with prefix is fine.

Operations can be arranged by moving units from positions with negative net change to positions with positive net change to their left. Since D_k≥0, the cumulative surplus to the left is enough. Let's maybe prove balanced max prefix using "for any k, Q_k ≤ Q*_k" with threshold B_k. Let's write it cleanly in case final explanation needed:
Let S=qN+r, 0≤r<N. Define C_i=q for i≤N-r, C_i=q+1 otherwise. For any nondecreasing integer B with sum S, fix k.
- If B_k ≤ q, then B_1,...,B_k ≤ q, so Q_k≤kq≤C_prefix.
- If B_k ≥ q+1, then B_{k+1},...,B_N ≥ q+1, so Q_k = S - (B_{k+1}+...+B_N) ≤ qN+r - (N-k)(q+1) = kq + k - N + r. This equals C_prefix if k>N-r, and is ≤kq=C_prefix if k≤N-r.
Thus Q_k≤C_prefix. Nice.

Let's decide code style:
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    ans = []
    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]
        idx += 1
        A = data[idx:idx+n]
        idx += n

        s = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(s, n)
        cnt_q = n - r  # number of elements equal to q in the balanced sequence

        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += A[k-1]
            if k <= cnt_q:
                limit = q * k
            else:
                limit = q * k + (k - cnt_q)
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break

        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
This is final.

Let's think if `data` could be empty? T>=1, no.

Potential issue: If we break early, we don't need to process remaining A, but we already sliced A. Good.
Potential issue: If T is large and sum N large, slicing A for each test creates many lists but total memory okay. Could avoid to be safe:
```
A = data[idx:idx+n]
idx += n
```
If N=2e5, A list 2e5 ints references? Actually slice creates new list of ints references, memory ~1.6 MB plus ints already in data. Fine.
Could avoid data list of ints and use iterator to reduce memory:
```
it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
T = next(it)
for _ in range(T):
    n = next(it)
    A = [next(it) for _ in range(n)]
...
```
This creates A list anyway. The split list of bytes exists temporarily but map lazy; however `sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()` creates list of all byte strings, memory maybe bigger but input small. `list(map(int,...))` creates ints list and discards bytes after? Actually map consumes split list, but split list exists until map completes; after list(map(...)) returns, bytes list freed. Both okay. Using data list is faster.

I recall the theorem: For nonnegative integer vectors with same total, B is reachable from A by moving mass left (without going negative) iff prefix sums of B >= prefix sums of A. This is true because the operation is "redistribution" and can be done greedily from right to left without negative. Let's prove by induction:
If A=B done. Let j be largest index with A_j > B_j? Since total same and prefix sums B>=A, there must be some i<j with A_i < B_i? Actually if A_j > B_j, surplus at j. Need move surplus left. Choose the rightmost? There is a deficit to the left because prefix sums. We can move one unit from j to some i<j where A_i < B_i. But if we move to i, A_i increases, may exceed B_i; but we can choose i as the rightmost deficit? Need maintain nonnegativity. Since A_j > B_j ≥0, A_j at least 1, so decrement safe. After moving, prefix sums between i and j-1 increase by1, so still >= original? We need maintain prefix sums >= A original? Actually we are transforming A towards B; after operation, prefix sums increase, so condition relative to original remains. But we need eventually reach B. This is like reducing difference. If we always move from a position with excess to a position with deficit to its left, the L1 distance to B decreases? Moving from j to i changes A_j down, A_i up. If A_j > B_j and A_i < B_i, then both get closer, so sum of absolute differences decreases by 2. Need ensure there exists such pair. If A≠B and prefix sums B>=A, total same, there is at least one index with A_i < B_i (deficit) and one with A_j > B_j (excess). Since prefix sums B>=A, the first index where A and B differ must be a deficit (A_i < B_i) because prefix B>=A and if A_i>B_i then prefix would be >? Actually if at first difference i, previous prefixes equal, then A_i≠B_i and prefix B_i>=A_i implies B_i>A_i, so deficit. Thus there is a deficit to the left of any excess? The first difference is deficit. For an excess at j, there is a deficit at some i<j? Since if all deficits are to the right of all excesses? Let's see prefix sums B>=A means cumulative surplus B-A is nonnegative. The cumulative difference starts 0, can go positive (deficit? Actually B-A positive means B has more mass in prefix, so A has deficit in prefix). At the end 0. If A_j > B_j at position j, then the individual difference at j is negative (A_j-B_j>0), so cumulative difference decreases at j. Since cumulative difference is nonnegative and ends 0, before j it must be positive, so there is some earlier position where B_i>A_i. Thus pair exists. Move from j to i. This maintains nonnegativity because A_j>B_j≥0. Repeating terminates. Thus sufficiency with nonnegative intermediate. Good. So even if negative not allowed, theorem holds. Nice.

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    out = []
    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]
        idx += 1
        A = data[idx:idx+n]
        idx += n

        s = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(s, n)
        cnt_q = n - r

        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += A[k-1]
            if k <= cnt_q:
                limit = q * k
            else:
                limit = q * k + (k - cnt_q)
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break

        out.append("Yes" if ok else "No")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's maybe write a formal proof of max prefix in my mind:
Let C be balanced. For any nondecreasing B, suppose for some k, Q_k > C_prefix. Since C_prefix is max, derive contradiction. Let x=B_k.
If x≤q, then Q_k≤kx≤kq≤C_prefix, contradiction. Thus x≥q+1.
Then B_{k+1},...,B_N≥x≥q+1, so suffix sum≥(N-k)(q+1). Hence Q_k≤S-(N-k)(q+1)=kq+k-N+r.
If k≤N-r, this is ≤kq=C_prefix. If k>N-r, this equals C_prefix. Contradiction. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    ans = []

    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]
        idx += 1

        A = data[idx:idx + n]
        idx += n

        s = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(s, n)

        # Balanced non-decreasing sequence:
        # q repeated (n-r) times, then q+1 repeated r times.
        cnt_q = n - r

        pref = 0
        ok = True

        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += A[k - 1]

            if k <= cnt_q:
                limit = q * k
            else:
                limit = q * k + (k - cnt_q)

            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break

        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
